The contradiction arises between the spatial description in **Evidence 8** and the direction given in **Testimony 4**.  
- **Evidence 8** states that Kay remembers a person coming *from beyond the concession stand* and pushing her off the building. The concession stand is described in the pamphlet (Evidence 7) as being at the right‑most edge of the viewing platform, so “beyond the stand” implies a direction from the right side of the platform (outside or beyond the stand).  
- **Testimony 4** says, “Umm… That’s right, someone was walking towards me from the other side of that stand.” The “other side” of the stand, given its position at the right edge, would be the left side of the platform.  

Thus, the two statements give opposite directions relative to the stand: one indicates the person came from the right side, the other from the left side. No spatial rule from the list applies, so the contradiction is identified purely by comparing the relative positions described.

These two statements cannot both be true because the gun cannot simultaneously be in Gina’s possession and in the owner’s possession at the same time.

**Step 2:**  
The spatial reasoning rules provided are about relative positions (above/below, left/right, inside). While the contradiction is about possession, we can interpret possession as a spatial relationship: “X is inside Y” (the gun is inside the person’s possession). Thus the contradiction can be seen as a conflict between two “inside” relationships.

**Step 3:**  
Rule 5: *If XXX is inside YYY and YYY is inside ZZZ then XXX is inside ZZZ.*  
Although this rule does not directly resolve the contradiction, it reinforces the idea that an object cannot be inside two distinct containers simultaneously. The gun being inside Gina’s possession contradicts it being inside the owner’s possession.

**Step 4:**  
The spatial reasoning used is the concept of exclusive containment: an object cannot be inside two separate entities at the same time. Therefore, the evidence that the gun was in Gina’s possession directly contradicts the testimony that it was in the owner’s possession.
